\"bot_command\" && e.Offset == 0\n250\t}\n251\t\n252\t// ExtractCommandAndArgs parses a bot command from the content text.\n253\t// It returns the command (e.g. \"/start\") and the remaining text as args.\n254\t// Returns (\"\", \"\") if the content is not a command.\n255\tfunc (c *Content) ExtractCommandAndArgs() (command, args string) {\n256\t\tif !c.IsCommand() {\n257\t\t\treturn \"\", \"\"\n258\t\t}\n259\t\te := c.Entities[0]\n260\t\trunes := []rune(*c.Text)\n261\t\tif e.Length > len(runes) {\n262\t\t\treturn \"\", \"\"\n263\t\t}\n264\t\tcommand = string(runes[:e.Length])\n265\t\t// Strip @BotName suffix if present (e.g. \"/start@mybot\" → \"/start\")\n266\t\tif idx := strings.IndexByte(command, '@'); idx != -1 {\n267\t\t\tcommand = command[:idx]\n268\t\t}\n269\t\targs = strings.TrimSpace(string(runes[e.Length:]))\n270\t\treturn command, args\n271\t}\n272\t\n273\t// SendPhotoRequest holds metadata for POST /send_photo restores full conversation context\n296-- `--append-system-prompt` — injects dispatcher context into orchestrator (see Phase 9)\n297-- `--disallowed-tools spawn_worker` — workers cannot recursively spawn\n298-- `--model` — per-session or per-worker model override\n299-- `--allowed-tools` / `--disallowed-tools` — tool restrictions per group\n--\n645----\n646-\n647-## Phase 2: Streaming and Formatting\n648-\n649-### 2.1 — Progressive Streaming\n650:- Use `--output-format stream-json --include-partial-messages` to get text deltas\n651-- Parse NDJSON lines from stdout, extract `text_delta` from `stream_event` objects\n652-- Send placeholder message, edit in-place as text streams\n653-- Debounce edits to 1/second\n654-- Handle overflow (>4096 chars) by sending new messages\n655-\n--\n658-- Code-block-aware message chunking\n659-- Oversized code blocks sent as document attachments\n660-\n661-### 2.3 — Permission Handling\n662-- Default `--permission-mode plan` means Claude proposes before executing\n663:- The CLI blocks on stdin waiting for approval — the bridge can detect tool approval prompts in the stream and send inline keyboards to Telegram\n664:- Callback query response writes approval/denial to the subprocess stdin\n665:- Alternative: use `--permission-mode acceptEdits` or `--permission-mode dontAsk` for trusted groups to skip interactive approval\n666-\n667:**Deliverable:** Streamed responses with proper formatting and interactive tool approval.\n668-\n669----\n670-\n671-## Phase 3: Media Processing\n672-\n--\n899-\n900-### 7.3 — Tool-use progress notifications\n901-\n902-**Problem:** During long agentic runs the user sees only \"Thinking…\" with no visibility into what Claude is doing.\n903-\n904:**Solution:** Parse `tool_use` and `tool_result` events from the stream-json NDJSON and send interim status messages to the topic.\n905-\n906-Stream-json emits `stream_event` lines containing `message_delta` and tool events. Parse:\n907-```json\n908-{\"type\":\"stream_event\",\"event\":{\"type\":\"content_block_start\",\"content_block\":{\"type\":\"tool_use\",\"name\":\"Bash\",\"input\":{\"command\":\"go test ./...\"}}}}\n909-```\n--\n1333-\n1334-| Decision | Options | Leaning | Notes |\n1335-|---|---|---|---|\n1336-| Proxy language | Go | **Decided: Go** | Same language as bridge, single binary, minimal container |\n1337-| Bridge language | Go | **Decided: Go** | See language evaluation below |\n1338:| Claude integration | Headless CLI | **Decided: CLI** | `claude -p` with `--output-format stream-json`, `--resume` for sessions. Subprocess per prompt. |\n1339-| Message format proxy→bridge | Normalized envelope vs raw Telegram JSON | Normalized | Keeps bridge decoupled from Telegram API specifics, proxy handles schema changes |\n1340-| Media transfer proxy→bridge | Inline in update vs separate `/file` endpoint | Separate `/file` | Keeps update payloads small, avoids large base64 blobs |\n1341-| Whisper model | turbo vs base vs small | turbo | Best accuracy-to-speed ratio |\n1342-| Private chat topics | Support vs groups only | Groups only (initially) | Bot API 9.4 supports private chat topics, but groups are the primary use case |\n1343-| Response format | HTML vs MarkdownV2 | HTML | MarkdownV2 escaping is unreliable — universal consensus from existing implementations |\n1344:| Permission mode | plan vs acceptEdits vs dontAsk | plan (default) | Configurable per group. `plan` gives interactive approval via Telegram inline keyboards. |\n1345-\n1346----\n1347-\n1348-## Language Evaluation\n1349-"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"call_a8f62a5469d24fbb8805b6ca","session_id":"3615c053-5998-4a9a-b6a0-573d164a84dd","uuid":"4df23778-2fa5-46eb-9902-1f8ae05d2914","timestamp":"2026-05-04T04:07:43.347Z"}

[Key Takeaways](#key-takeaways)\n24\t\n25\t---\n26\t\n27\t## Individual Project Summaries\n28\t\n29\t### Claude Code-Specific Bridges\n30\t\n31\t#### 1. terranc/claude-telegram-bot-bridge\n32\t\n33\t- **URL**: https://github.com/terranc/claude-telegram-bot-bridge\n34\t- **Architecture**: Python daemon using the Claude Code SDK directly. Maintains per-user dedicated SDK streams (up to 3 concurrent per user). Polling-based (no HTTP exposure). Stores runtime state under `PROJECT_ROOT/.telegram_bot/`.\n35\t- **Language**: Python 3.11+\n36\t- **Key features**:\n37\t  - Real-time progressive streaming of Claude responses\n38\t  - Numbered options converted to Telegram inline keyboard buttons\n39\t  - Voice message support (Whisper or Volcengine ASR via ffmpeg)\n40\t  - Five revert modes (full restore, conversation-only, code-only, summarize, cancel)\n41\t  - Session resumption with browsable history and pagination\n42\t  - Runtime model switching (Sonnet, Opus, Haiku)\n43\t  - Priority command queue for `/stop` and `/revert` that bypass normal message limits\n44\t  - Sandbox enforcement: project-directory access auto-allowed, external access requires inline-button confirmation\n45\t  - Messages older than 20 minutes silently dropped (staleness filter)\n46\t  - Crash recovery daemon with 5-crash/60-second circuit breaker\n47\t  - 14-day log rotation\n48\t  - macOS launchd autostart support\n49\t\n50\t---\n51\t\n52\t#### 2. six-ddc/ccbot\n53\t\n54\t- **URL**: https://github.com/six-ddc/ccbot\n55\t- **Architecture**: Thin tmux control layer. Does NOT wrap the SDK -- reads terminal output and sends keystrokes via tmux. Each Telegram Forum topic maps 1:1 to a tmux window running a Claude Code instance. JSONL file polling at 2-second intervals for session monitoring. Hook-based session tracking via Claude's SessionStart hook.\n56\t- **Language**: Python\n57\t- **Key features**:\n58\t  - Topic-based 1:1 mapping (1 topic = 1 window = 1 session)\n59\t  - Terminal remains source of truth; seamless switch between Telegram and desktop terminal\n60\t  - Interactive UI handling for AskUserQuestion, ExitPlanMode, Permission prompts\n61\t  - Directory browser with inline keyboard navigation\n62\t  - Terminal screenshot capture with ANSI color support\n63\t  - Voice transcription via OpenAI Whisper\n64\t  - Hook-based automatic session registration (no manual setup)\n65\t  - HTML fixed parse mode avoids MarkdownV2 complexity\n66\t  - Tag-aware message splitting preserves code block integrity\n67\t  - Per-user read offset tracking prevents duplicate notifications\n68\t\n69\t---\n70\t\n71\t#### 3. RichardAtCT/claude-code-telegram\n72\t\n73\t- **URL**: https://github.com/RichardAtCT/claude-code-telegram\n74\t- **Architecture**: Event-bus architecture with dual mode: Agentic (default, natural language) and Classic (terminal-like with 13 explicit commands). Claude SDK primary, CLI fallback. SQLite with migrations for persistent storage. Optional FastAPI webhook server.\n75\t- **Language**: Python 3.11+ (Poetry)\n76\t- **Key features**:\n77\t  - 16 configurable tools with allowlist/disallowlist control\n78\t  - Webhook API server with GitHub HMAC-SHA256 and Bearer token auth\n79\t  - Cron job scheduler with persistent storage\n80\t  - Per-chat rate limiting via token bucket algorithm\n81\t  - Directory sandboxing with path traversal prevention\n82\t  - Per-user spending limits (`CLAUDE_MAX_COST_PER_USER`)\n83\t  - Complete audit logging of all user actions\n84\t  - Session export in Markdown, HTML, JSON\n85\t  - Quick actions system with context-aware buttons\n86\t  - DDD with clean architecture (Domain, Application, Infrastructure, Presentation layers)\n87\t  - 143+ unit tests\n88\t  - Docker Compose deployment with interactive deploy script\n89\t\n90\t---\n91\t\n92\t#### 4. avivsinai/telclaude\n93\t\n94\t- **URL**: https://github.com/avivsinai/telclaude\n95\t- **Architecture**: Multi-layer security relay between Telegram and Claude Agent SDK. Messages pass through: (1) regex-based secret detection, (2) Haiku LLM observer for semantic screening, (3) rate limiting, (4) human approval for FULL_ACCESS tier, (5) permission tier enforcement, (6) SDK sandbox or Docker container firewall.\n96\t- **Language**: TypeScript (Node.js 20+, pnpm)\n97\t- **Key features**:\n98\t  - Four permission tiers: READ_ONLY, WRITE_LOCAL, SOCIAL, FULL_ACCESS\n99\t  - Credential vault sidecar: AES-256-GCM encrypted at rest, HTTP proxy injects auth headers, agents never see raw credentials\n100\t  - TOTP-gated periodic re-authentication\n101\t  - Nonce-based human approval workflow for FULL_ACCESS requests\n102\t  - Fail-closed defaults (empty allowlist = deny all, `defaultTier=FULL_ACCESS` rejected at runtime)\n103\t  - Secret redaction with CORE pattern matching + entropy detection\n104\t  - Docker Compose recommended: 6-container stack (relay, agent, social persona, google-services sidecar, TOTP daemon, vault daemon)\n105\t  - Social services integration (Twitter/X, Bluesky)\n106\t  - Private network CIDR/host allowlist for homelab services\n107\t  - Destructive bash command blocking in WRITE_LOCAL tier\n108\t  - Socket permissions enforced (0600)\n109\t  - No telemetry; audit logs only\n110\t\n111\t---\n112\t\n113\t#### 5. hanxiao/claudecode-telegram\n114\t\n115\t- **URL**: https://github.com/hanxiao/claudecode-telegram\n116\t- **Architecture**: Python bridge using tmux send-keys for message injection. Webhook-based Telegram integration via Cloudflare Tunnel. Claude Code's Stop hook reads transcript and sends response back to Telegram.\n117\t- **Language**: Python (77.7%), Shell (22.3%)\n118\t- **Key features**:\n119\t  - Cloudflare Tunnel avoids public IP exposure for webhooks\n120\t  - Pending file flag for state management (filesystem-based)\n121\t  - Bot commands: `/status`, `/clear`, `/resume`, `/continue_`, `/loop`, `/stop`\n122\t  - Session management and resumption\n123\t  - Minimal design relying on Claude Code's native hook system\n124\t\n125\t---\n126\t\n127\t#### 6. andrueandersoncs/telegram-claude\n128\t\n129\t- **URL**: https://github.com/andrueandersoncs/telegram-claude\n130\t- **Architecture**: TypeScript service with four components: TelegramService, ClaudeSessionService (Agent SDK lifecycle), PersistenceService (SQLite for session-to-chat mappings), NotificationService. Each Telegram chat maps to a distinct Claude Code session.\n131\t- **Language**: TypeScript with Effect library for type-safe async composition\n132\t- **Key features**:\n133\t  - SQLite persistence surviving service restarts\n134\t  - Image analysis support\n135\t  - Formatted tool call visibility\n136\t  - Automatic context handoff at token limits\n137\t  - Natural conversation flow (no slash commands needed)\n138\t  - Single-user restriction by design (private bot)\n139\t  - Directory sandboxing via `WORKSPACE_PATH`\n140\t\n141\t---\n142\t\n143\t#### 7. gergomiklos/heyagent\n144\t\n145\t- **URL**: https://github.com/gergomiklos/heyagent\n146\t- **Architecture**: Polling-based bridge (no webhooks). One chat per running process. Messages queued and grouped during active execution, then batched into next run. Local-first with config at `~/.heyagent/config.json`.\n147\t- **Language**: JavaScript/Node.js\n148\t- **Key features**:\n149\t  - Supports both Claude Code and Codex CLI with mid-session provider switching\n150\t  - QR code-based phone setup (Cloudflare Quick Tunnel)\n151\t  - Message batching during active execution reduces API calls\n152\t  - Sleep prevention during operation\n153\t  - Configurable permission modes per provider\n154\t  - Local CLI input alongside Telegram input\n155\t  - No external server dependency\n156\t\n157\t---\n158\t\n159\t#### 8. NachoSEO/claudegram\n160\t\n161\t- **URL**: https://github.com/NachoSEO/claudegram\n162\t- **Architecture**: TypeScript with Grammy bot framework and Claude Agent SDK. MCP (Model Context Protocol) for tool routing. Per-chat session state with model selection, streaming mode, TTS voice preferences.\n163\t- **Language**: TypeScript (Node.js 18+)\n164\t- **Key features**:\n165\t  - Full Claude Code tool access (Bash, Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep)\n166\t  - Three execution modes: plan, explore, loop\n167\t  - Reddit/Medium/YouTube integrations via MCP tools\n168\t  - Voice transcription (Groq Whisper) and TTS (OpenAI, 13 voices)\n169\t  - Telegraph Instant View for long responses and tables\n170\t  - Smart code-block-aware chunking\n171\t  - Terminal-style UI with tool status spinners\n172\t  - ForceReply interactive prompts\n173\t  - Forum topic sessions for parallel projects\n174\t  - Watchdog system for long-running tasks\n175\t  - Request queue for sequential processing (prevents race conditions)\n176\t  - Stale message filtering on restart\n177\t  - Message deduplication\n178\t  - macOS caffeinate utility to prevent sleep\n179\t\n180\t---\n181\t\n182\t#### 9. op7418/Claude-to-IM-skill & Claude-to-IM\n183\t\n184\t- **URL (skill)**: https://github.com/op7418/Claude-to-IM-skill\n185\t- **URL (library)**: https://github.com/op7418/Claude-to-IM\n186\t- **Architecture**: The skill version is a local Node.js daemon bridging multiple IM platforms (Telegram, Discord, Feishu, QQ, WeChat). The library version is a dependency-injection bridge extracted from CodePilot (a desktop GUI client) with four DI interfaces: BridgeStore, LLMProvider, PermissionGateway, LifecycleHooks.\n187\t- **Language**: TypeScript\n188\t- **Key features**:\n189\t  - Multi-platform support (5 IM platforms)\n190\t  - Interactive setup wizard with token validation\n191\t  - Permission control via inline buttons (Telegram/Discord) or text commands\n192\t  - Streaming response preview with throttling\n193\t  - Token bucket rate limiting (20 msg/min per chat)\n194\t  - Exponential backoff retry for delivery\n195\t  - HTML fallback when Markdown parsing fails\n196\t  - Message deduplication\n197\t  - `doctor` diagnostic command checking Node version, config, tokens, logs, PID\n198\t  - Credentials stored with chmod 600 file permissions\n199\t  - Automatic token redaction in all log output\n200\t  - No bundled persistence (you implement ~30 BridgeStore methods)\n201\t\n202\t---\n203\t\n204\t#### 10. viniciustodesco/claude-telegram-bridge\n205\t\n206\t- **URL**: https://github.com/viniciustodesco/claude-telegram-bridge\n207\t- **Architecture**: Node.js spawning Claude Code CLI as subprocess with `--print --output-format stream-json`. Debounced streaming. UUID-based session IDs for context maintenance.\n208\t- **Language**: JavaScript (Node.js 18+)\n209\t- **Key features**:\n210\t  - Real-time streaming with progressive message updates\n211\t  - Image analysis via Anthropic vision API\n212\t  - Voice transcription via Whisper\n213\t  - Multi-language support (English, Portuguese, Dutch) with dynamic switching\n214\t  - Chat ID-based authentication\n215\t  - Group chat support with shared sessions\n216\t  - Graceful degradation (works without OpenAI key, just no transcription)\n217\t\n218\t---\n219\t\n220\t#### 11. AliceLJY/telegram-cli-bridge\n221\t\n222\t- **URL**: https://github.com/AliceLJY/telegram-cli-bridge\n223\t- **Architecture**: Thin Telegram frontend for task-api. Forwards tasks to task-api endpoints, polls for results. Delegates execution to openclaw-tunnel (Docker-to-host CLI bridge). Three separate bot scripts (not unified).\n224\t- **Language**: TypeScript (Bun runtime)\n225\t- **Key features**:\n226\t  - Supports Claude Code, Codex CLI, and Gemini CLI\n227\t  - Owner-only access restriction\n228\t  - Per-chat in-memory sessions (lost on restart)\n229\t  - Media forwarding (files, photos, voice)\n230\t\n231\t---\n232\t\n233\t#### 12. n4rly-boop/OpenClaude\n234\t\n235\t- **URL**: https://github.com/n4rly-boop/OpenClaude\n236\t- **Architecture**: Hexagonal (ports & adapters) architecture. Domain objects, abstract protocols, use cases, and repositories separated from infrastructure. Supports both SDK and subprocess backends.\n237\t- **Language**: Python 3.11+\n238\t- **Key features**:\n239\t  - Streaming live updates with tool usage indicators\n240\t  - Per-user workspace isolation (`workspaces/c{chat_id}/`)\n241\t  - Three-tier memory system (workspace-wide, per-topic, daily logs)\n242\t  - LaTeX rendering via KaTeX with matplotlib fallback\n243\t  - Ouroboros watchdog daemon auto-restarting dead processes (30-second interval)\n244\t  - Safe restart mechanism waiting for active streams\n245\t  - Message batching for rapid-fire inputs\n246\t  - Graceful splitting at paragraph boundaries\n247\t  - 218 automated tests with GitHub Actions CI\n248\t  - Guard hooks (`guard.sh`, `guard-write.sh`) enforce security at subprocess level\n249\t  - Universally blocked commands: systemctl, kill, SSH/firewall/auth modifications\n250\t  - Non-admin restrictions: no host env access, no credential file reading, workspace-confined\n251\t  - Restart recovery notifications\n252\t\n253\t---\n254\t\n255\t#### 13. Angusstone7/claude-code-telegram\n256\t\n257\t- **URL**: https://github.com/Angusstone7/claude-code-telegram\n258\t- **Architecture**: Python with DDD clean architecture. Claude Code SDK primary with CLI fallback. TypeScript MCP server for Telegram tools. SQLite persistence.\n259\t- **Language**: Python 3.11+\n260\t- **Key features**:\n261\t  - Human-in-the-loop tool approval buttons (Approve/Deny/View)\n262\t  - YOLO mode for auto-approving actions\n263\t  - Plan review before execution\n264\t  - Multi-project support with seamless switching\n265\t  - File browser navigation via Telegram UI\n266\t  - Docker container management and system monitoring (CPU, memory, disk)\n267\t  - Docker Compose with production-ready config\n268\t  - ZhipuAI compatibility for China regions\n269\t\n270\t---\n271\t\n272\t### Official Anthropic Plugin\n273\t\n274\t#### anthropics/claude-plugins-official (Telegram)\n275\t\n276\t- **URL**: https://github.com/anthropics/claude-plugins-official/tree/main/external_plugins/telegram\n277\t- **Architecture**: MCP (Model Context Protocol) server running as a Bun process. Logs into Telegram as a bot and exposes tools (`reply`, `react`, `edit_message`) to Claude Code. Inbound messages forwarded to Claude Code session. Launched with `claude --channels plugin:telegram@claude-plugins-official`.\n278\t- **Language**: TypeScript (Bun runtime)\n279\t- **Key features**:\n280\t  - Three tools exposed to Claude: `reply` (with threading and file attachments up to 50MB), `react` (emoji reactions), `edit_message` (progress updates)\n281\t  - Pairing-code authentication (6-character code via DM)\n282\t  - Two access policies: `pairing` (default, any user gets pairing code) and `allowlist` (numeric Telegram user IDs only)\n283\t  - Automatic typing indicator while Claude processes\n284\t  - Eager photo download on arrival (Telegram Bot API has no history access)\n285\t  - Auto-chunking for long responses with configurable threading mode\n286\t  - State stored in `~/.claude/channels/telegram/`\n287\t  - Multi-bot support via `TELEGRAM_STATE_DIR`\n288\t  - No message history access (Telegram Bot API limitation)\n289\t  - Image formats render as photos; others as documents\n290\t\n291\t---\n292\t\n293\t### General Claude/Anthropic Bots\n294\t\n295\t#### llegomark/claude-anthropic-telegram-bot\n296\t\n297\t- **URL**: https://github.com/llegomark/claude-anthropic-telegram-bot\n298\t- **Architecture**: Direct Anthropic API integration. Modular Python with separate bot, API, auth, scenarios, and utils modules.\n299\t- **Language**: Python 3.12+\n300\t- **Key features**:\n301\t  - 9 conversation scenarios (Mentor, Coach, Socratic Tutor, etc.)\n302\t  - Simple auth code mechanism\n303\t  - Local conversation history storage\n304\t  - Explicitly marked \"not recommended for production use\"\n305\t\n306\t#### FlamingoFiesta/Chatgpt-Claude_telegram_bot_with_payment_system\n307\t\n308\t- **URL**: https://github.com/FlamingoFiesta/Chatgpt-Claude_telegram_bot_with_payment_system\n309\t- **Architecture**: Python with Flask webhook server. Docker Compose deployment. Stripe payment integration.\n310\t- **Language**: Python\n311\t- **Key features**:\n312\t  - Dual API support (OpenAI + Anthropic simultaneously)\n313\t  - Stripe payment system with automatic balance updates via webhook\n314\t  - User role-based pricing (admin, beta tester, friend, regular, trial)\n315\t  - Configurable profit margins\n316\t  - Trial users get starting balance; auto-upgrade after first payment\n317\t  - Pre-API-call balance validation\n318\t  - 30+ specialized chat modes\n319\t  - Docker Compose with payment/no-payment variants\n320\t\n321\t---\n322\t\n323\t### ChatGPT/Multi-LLM Telegram Bots\n324\t\n325\tThese are included because they represent mature implementations with transferable patterns.\n326\t\n327\t#### n3d1117/chatgpt-telegram-bot\n328\t\n329\t- **URL**: https://github.com/n3d1117/chatgpt-telegram-bot\n330\t- **Architecture**: Python with python-telegram-bot. Plugin architecture supporting function calling. In-memory conversation state with automatic summarization at token limits.\n331\t- **Language**: Python\n332\t- **Key features**:\n333\t  - Admin/user role separation with different allowlists\n334\t  - Per-user budget system (daily, monthly, all-time) with token-level price tracking\n335\t  - Guest budget enforcement in group chats\n336\t  - 16 plugins (weather, Wolfram Alpha, DuckDuckGo, Spotify, crypto, etc.)\n337\t  - Streaming enabled by default\n338\t  - Conversation summarization when memory limit reached (cost-controlled)\n339\t  - Session age limit (default 180 minutes)\n340\t  - Docker Compose + Heroku deployment\n341\t  - 20+ language localizations\n342\t  - 50+ configuration parameters\n343\t\n344\t#### yym68686/ChatGPT-Telegram-Bot (TeleChat)\n345\t\n346\t- **URL**: https://github.com/yym68686/ChatGPT-Telegram-Bot\n347\t- **Architecture**: Python async with custom `aient` SDK submodule (not official OpenAI client). Plugin-based. File-based config storage.\n348\t- **Language**: Python\n349\t- **Key features**:\n350\t  - Supports OpenAI, Claude, Gemini models via unified interface\n351\t  - Model grouping system (`GPT:model1,model2;Claude:model3`)\n352\t  - Auto-merging of long input messages (bypasses Telegram's length limit)\n353\t  - Output splitting for oversized responses\n354\t  - Streaming/typewriter-effect rendering\n355\t  - Per-user or global configuration modes\n356\t  - Topic-based isolation in group chats\n357\t  - Persistent config storage surviving restarts\n358\t  - One-click deployment on Koyeb, Zeabur, Replit, fly.io\n359\t  - Custom Markdown-to-Telegram converter (`md2tgmd` submodule)\n360\t\n361\t#### F33RNI/GPT-Telegramus\n362\t\n363\t- **URL**: https://github.com/F33RNI/GPT-Telegramus\n364\t- **Architecture**: Python with queue-based request processing. Modular backend wrapper supporting multiple AI providers. JSON-based configuration.\n365\t- **Language**: Python 3.10+\n366\t- **Key features**:\n367\t  - Multi-backend: ChatGPT, Microsoft Copilot, Gemini, Groq\n368\t  - Queue system decouples request processing from user interaction\n369\t  - Admin commands: ban/unban, queue monitoring, module restart, broadcast messaging\n370\t  - Stream writing support\n371\t  - Dynamic code block splitting\n372\t  - 11 language translations\n373\t  - Systemd service templates, Raspberry Pi/ARM support\n374\t  - AGPL-3.0 license (copyleft)\n375\t\n376\t---\n377\t\n378\t## Cross-Cutting Analysis\n379\t\n380\t### Security Patterns\n381\t\n382\t#### User Authentication and Authorization\n383\t\n384\t| Pattern | Prevalence | Examples |\n385\t|---------|-----------|----------|\n386\t| **Telegram user ID allowlist** | Nearly universal | All Claude bridges, n3d1117, yym68686, F33RNI |\n387\t| **Fail-closed defaults** (empty allowlist = deny all) | telclaude | Explicit rejection of permissive defaults |\n388\t| **Admin vs user role separation** | Common | n3d1117 (ADMIN_USER_IDS), OpenClaude (first user = admin), RichardAtCT |\n389\t| **Pairing code flow** | Official plugin | 6-character code via DM, then switch to allowlist |\n390\t| **TOTP re-authentication** | telclaude | Periodic identity verification for high-trust tiers |\n391\t| **Single auth code** | llegomark | Simple shared password (not production-grade) |\n392\t| **Permission tiers** | telclaude | READ_ONLY / WRITE_LOCAL / SOCIAL / FULL_ACCESS with tool-level granularity |\n393\t\n394\t**Consensus**: Telegram user ID allowlist is the baseline. More security-conscious projects layer tiered permissions and periodic re-authentication on top.\n395\t\n396\t#### Token and Secret Management\n397\t\n398\t| Pattern | Examples |\n399\t|---------|----------|\n400\t| Environment variables via `.env` file | Universal |\n401\t| Credential vault sidecar with AES-256-GCM encryption | telclaude |\n402\t| Agents never see raw credentials (HTTP proxy injection) | telclaude |\n403\t| chmod 600 on config files | Claude-to-IM-skill |\n404\t| Token redaction in all log output | Claude-to-IM-skill, telclaude |\n405\t| `.gitignore` for credential files | Universal |\n406\t\n407\t**Consensus**: `.env` files are the norm. Only telclaude goes further with encrypted vaults and proxy-based credential injection -- a pattern worth noting for multi-user or shared-host deployments.\n408\t\n409\t#### Rate Limiting\n410\t\n411\t| Pattern | Examples |\n412\t|---------|----------|\n413\t| Token bucket per chat | RichardAtCT, Claude-to-IM (20 msg/min) |\n414\t| Per-user spending limits | RichardAtCT (`CLAUDE_MAX_COST_PER_USER`), n3d1117 (daily/monthly/all-time budgets) |\n415\t| Telegram API flood limit compliance | python-telegram-bot's built-in AIORateLimiter (30 msg/s global, 20 msg/min per group) |\n416\t| Message staleness filter | terranc (20-minute cutoff), claudegram (stale message filtering on restart) |\n417\t\n418\t**Consensus**: Rate limiting is implemented inconsistently. Spending caps and staleness filters are more common than request-rate limiters. The Telegram API's own flood limits are a hard constraint that most projects handle implicitly.\n419\t\n420\t#### Input Sanitization and Prompt Injection Prevention\n421\t\n422\t| Pattern | Examples |\n423\t|---------|----------|\n424\t| Regex-based secret detection | telclaude (fast-path filter) |\n425\t| LLM observer pre-screening | telclaude (Haiku observer for semantic analysis) |\n426\t| Directory sandboxing / path traversal prevention | RichardAtCT, OpenClaude, andrueandersoncs |\n427\t| Blocked dangerous commands | OpenClaude (systemctl, kill, SSH), telclaude (rm, chown, kill in WRITE_LOCAL) |\n428\t| Guard hooks at subprocess level | OpenClaude (guard.sh, guard-write.sh) |\n429\t| SSRF protection (URL validation) | claudegram |\n430\t\n431\t**Consensus**: Most projects rely on directory sandboxing and command allowlists rather than prompt injection defenses. Only telclaude implements LLM-based semantic screening. This is a significant gap -- since these bots give Claude Code shell access, prompt injection through user messages could be weaponized.\n432\t\n433\t#### Admin Commands vs User Commands\n434\t\n435\tMost projects separate admin-only operations:\n436\t- **Admin**: ban/unban, broadcast, queue monitoring, module restart, role management, spending limit adjustment\n437\t- **User**: new chat, history, help, model selection, status, stop\n438\t\n439\t---\n440\t\n441\t### Convenience Features\n442\t\n443\t#### Multi-turn Conversation Support\n444\t\n445\tEvery project supports multi-turn conversations. Approaches vary:\n446\t\n447\t| Approach | Examples |\n448\t|----------|----------|\n449\t| SDK session persistence (conversation ID) | terranc, RichardAtCT, andrueandersoncs, claudegram |\n450\t| tmux session continuity | ccbot, hanxiao |\n451\t| Claude `--resume` flag | OpenClaude |\n452\t| In-memory with summarization at token limit | n3d1117 |\n453\t| File-based conversation history | llegomark, yym68686 |\n454\t\n455\t#### Context and Conversation Management\n456\t\n457\t| Feature | Examples |\n458\t|---------|----------|\n459\t| `/clear` or `/new` to reset | Nearly universal |\n460\t| Session resumption with browsable history | terranc (paginated), ccbot |\n461\t| Session export (Markdown, HTML, JSON) | RichardAtCT |\n462\t| Three-tier memory (workspace, topic, daily) | OpenClaude |\n463\t| Configurable history retention | yym68686 (`PASS_HISTORY`), n3d1117 (max messages + summarization) |\n464\t| Session age limits | n3d1117 (180-minute default) |\n465\t\n466\t#### Model Selection\n467\t\n468\t| Feature | Examples |\n469\t|---------|----------|\n470\t| Runtime model switching | terranc (Sonnet/Opus/Haiku), claudegram, Angusstone7 |\n471\t| Model grouping with provider labels | yym68686 (`GPT:model1;Claude:model2`) |\n472\t| Per-chat model preference storage | claudegram |\n473\t\n474\t#### System Prompt Customization\n475\t\n476\t| Feature | Examples |\n477\t|---------|----------|\n478\t| Conversation scenarios/modes | llegomark (9 scenarios), n3d1117 (chat modes), FlamingoFiesta (30+ modes) |\n479\t| Per-project context | RichardAtCT (per user/project session), OpenClaude (per-topic memory) |\n480\t\n481\t#### Image/Voice/Document Handling\n482\t\n483\t| Feature | Examples |\n484\t|---------|----------|\n485\t| Voice message transcription | terranc (Whisper/Volcengine), ccbot (Whisper), claudegram (Groq Whisper), viniciustodesco (Whisper) |\n486\t| Text-to-speech responses | claudegram (OpenAI TTS, 13 voices), terranc (macOS voice) |\n487\t| Image analysis | RichardAtCT, andrueandersoncs, viniciustodesco (Anthropic vision) |\n488\t| File upload handling | terranc (images, PDFs as documents), RichardAtCT (archive extraction) |\n489\t| Eager photo download | Official plugin (Telegram Bot API has no history/retrieval) |\n490\t\n491\t#### Markdown Rendering\n492\t\n493\t| Approach | Examples |\n494\t|----------|----------|\n495\t| MarkdownV2 with auto-escaping | claudegram |\n496\t| Fixed HTML parse mode (avoids MarkdownV2 complexity) | ccbot |\n497\t| Custom md-to-Telegram converter | yym68686 (`md2tgmd`), ccbot (`chatgpt-md-converter`) |\n498\t| HTML fallback on Markdown parse errors | Claude-to-IM |\n499\t| Telegraph Instant View for long responses | claudegram |\n500\t\n501\t**Note**: MarkdownV2 is notoriously difficult in the Telegram Bot API. Many projects avoid it entirely in favor of HTML, or build custom converters. This is a common source of bugs.\n502\t\n503\t#### Streaming and Progressive Updates\n504\t\n505\t| Approach | Examples |\n506\t|----------|----------|\n507\t| Edit-in-place with debouncing | terranc (150 chars / 1.0s interval), viniciustodesco |\n508\t| Streaming with throttled preview edits | Claude-to-IM, claudegram |\n509\t| Typewriter-effect rendering | yym68686 |\n510\t| Tool status spinners | claudegram (terminal-style UI) |\n511\t| Persistent typing indicators | RichardAtCT |\n512\t\n513\t**Consensus**: Progressive message editing is the standard approach. Debouncing/throttling is essential to avoid Telegram's rate limits on message edits.\n514\t\n515\t---\n516\t\n517\t### Resilience and Error Handling\n518\t\n519\t#### Retry Logic for API Failures\n520\t\n521\t| Pattern | Examples |\n522\t|---------|----------|\n523\t| Exponential backoff retry for delivery | Claude-to-IM |\n524\t| Docker restart policies | yym68686 (`--restart unless-stopped`) |\n525\t| Daemon auto-restart with circuit breaker | terranc (5 crashes in 60s stops restart) |\n526\t| Ouroboros watchdog daemon | OpenClaude (30-second interval, auto-restart) |\n527\t| Module restart without full bot reload | GPT-Telegramus |\n528\t\n529\t#### Timeout Handling for Long Responses\n530\t\n531\t| Pattern | Examples |\n532\t|---------|----------|\n533\t| Configurable process timeout | terranc (600s), RichardAtCT (300s), Angusstone7 (600s) |\n534\t| Watchdog for long-running tasks | claudegram |\n535\t| Permission request timeout with auto-denial | Claude-to-IM-skill (5 minutes) |\n536\t\n537\t#### Graceful Degradation\n538\t\n539\t| Pattern | Examples |\n540\t|---------|----------|\n541\t| SDK primary, CLI fallback | RichardAtCT, Angusstone7, OpenClaude |\n542\t| Works without optional API keys | viniciustodesco (no Whisper without OpenAI key) |\n543\t| HTML fallback on Markdown parse errors | Claude-to-IM |\n544\t| Missing optional integrations don't break core | claudegram |\n545\t\n546\t#### Queue Management for Concurrent Requests\n547\t\n548\t| Pattern | Examples |\n549\t|---------|----------|\n550\t| Sequential request queue | claudegram (prevents race conditions) |\n551\t| Per-user message queuing with rate limiting | ccbot |\n552\t| Queue-based async processing | GPT-Telegramus |\n553\t| Priority command queue (bypass normal limits) | terranc (`/stop`, `/revert`) |\n554\t| Message batching during active execution | heyagent |\n555\t\n556\t#### Connection Recovery\n557\t\n558\t| Pattern | Examples |\n559\t|---------|----------|\n560\t| Polling-based (inherently resilient) | terranc, heyagent, ccbot, Claude-to-IM |\n561\t| Webhook + Cloudflare Tunnel | hanxiao |\n562\t| Dedicated HTTP/1.1 polling client with proxy awareness | terranc |\n563\t| Polling and webhook fallback | yym68686 |\n564\t\n565\t**Consensus**: Polling is overwhelmingly preferred over webhooks. It avoids HTTP exposure, works behind NAT/firewalls, and recovers automatically from network interruptions.\n566\t\n567\t#### State Persistence (Surviving Restarts)\n568\t\n569\t| Approach | Examples |\n570\t|----------|----------|\n571\t| SQLite | RichardAtCT, andrueandersoncs, Angusstone7 |\n572\t| JSON files | ccbot (atomic JSON writes), OpenClaude, Claude-to-IM-skill |\n573\t| File-based config | yym68686 (user_configs directory) |\n574\t| In-memory only (lost on restart) | telegram-cli-bridge, n3d1117 (with summarization) |\n575\t| Filesystem state (`~/.ccbot/`, `~/.heyagent/`) | ccbot, heyagent |\n576\t| Claude `--resume` flag for session continuity | OpenClaude |\n577\t\n578\t---\n579\t\n580\t### Robustness and Operations\n581\t\n582\t#### Logging and Monitoring\n583\t\n584\t| Pattern | Examples |\n585\t|---------|----------|\n586\t| 14-day log rotation | terranc |\n587\t| Audit logging of all user actions | RichardAtCT, telclaude |\n588\t| Token redaction in logs | Claude-to-IM-skill, telclaude |\n589\t| Request/response file logging | GPT-Telegramus |\n590\t| Tool use/result summarization with statistics | ccbot |\n591\t\n592\t#### Health Checks and Diagnostics\n593\t\n594\t| Pattern | Examples |\n595\t|---------|----------|\n596\t| `/status` command | Nearly universal |\n597\t| `doctor` diagnostic command | Claude-to-IM-skill (checks Node version, config permissions, token validity, log directory, PID) |\n598\t| System monitoring (CPU, memory, disk) | Angusstone7 |\n599\t| Admin queue monitoring | GPT-Telegramus |\n600\t\n601\t#### Containerization\n602\t\n603\t| Pattern | Examples |\n604\t|---------|----------|\n605\t| Docker Compose production deployment | RichardAtCT, Angusstone7, FlamingoFiesta, n3d1117 |\n606\t| 6-container Docker Compose stack | telclaude |\n607\t| No Docker (native install) | terranc, ccbot, heyagent, claudegram, OpenClaude |\n608\t| One-click cloud deployment (Koyeb, Zeabur, fly.io) | yym68686 |\n609\t| Systemd service templates | GPT-Telegramus, OpenClaude |\n610\t| macOS launchd plist | terranc |\n611\t\n612\t#### Configuration Management\n613\t\n614\t| Approach | Prevalence |\n615\t|----------|-----------|\n616\t| Environment variables (`.env`) | Universal |\n617\t| JSON config files | GPT-Telegramus, ccbot, Claude-to-IM-skill |\n618\t| YAML config | FlamingoFiesta |\n619\t| JSON5 config | telclaude |\n620\t| Poetry/pyproject.toml | RichardAtCT |\n621\t| pnpm workspaces | telclaude |\n622\t\n623\t#### Message Chunking for Long Responses\n624\t\n625\t| Approach | Examples |\n626\t|----------|----------|\n627\t| Auto-split at Telegram's 4096-char limit | Official plugin, Claude-to-IM |\n628\t| Code-block-aware splitting | ccbot, GPT-Telegramus |\n629\t| Paragraph-boundary splitting | OpenClaude |\n630\t| Telegraph Instant View for very long content | claudegram |\n631\t| Configurable chunk limit | Official plugin (`textChunkLimit`) |\n632\t\n633\t**Consensus**: Naive character-limit splitting breaks code blocks and formatting. The best implementations split at paragraph or code-block boundaries. Telegraph Instant View is a creative escape hatch for truly long content.\n634\t\n635\t#### Cost Tracking and Usage Limits\n636\t\n637\t| Pattern | Examples |\n638\t|---------|----------|\n639\t| Per-user spending limits | RichardAtCT (`CLAUDE_MAX_COST_PER_USER`) |\n640\t| Token-level budget tracking (daily/monthly/all-time) | n3d1117 |\n641\t| Role-based pricing with profit margins | FlamingoFiesta |\n642\t| Pre-API-call balance validation | FlamingoFiesta |\n643\t\n644\t---\n645\t\n646\t### Notable Design Decisions\n647\t\n648\t#### Architecture Spectrum\n649\t\n650\tProjects fall into four architectural categories (see [Architecture Taxonomy](#architecture-taxonomy) below):\n651\t\n652\t1. **SDK-direct**: Use Claude Code SDK/Agent SDK as a library (terranc, RichardAtCT, telclaude, andrueandersoncs, claudegram, OpenClaude, Angusstone7)\n653\t2. **CLI subprocess**: Spawn `claude` CLI and parse stream-json output (viniciustodesco)\n654\t3. **tmux bridge**: Read/write to tmux sessions running Claude Code (ccbot, hanxiao)\n655\t4. **Task API delegation**: Forward to external task-api service (telegram-cli-bridge)\n656\t\n657\t#### Creative or Unusual Approaches\n658\t\n659\t- **telclaude's LLM pre-screening**: Using a cheap/fast model (Haiku) to semantically screen incoming messages before forwarding to the main agent. This catches prompt injection and policy violations that regex alone would miss.\n660\t- **ccbot's tmux architecture**: By operating on tmux rather than the SDK, it preserves full terminal context and allows seamless desktop/mobile switching. The terminal is always the source of truth.\n661\t- **claudegram's Telegraph Instant View**: Routing long responses to Telegraph articles solves the Telegram message length problem elegantly while maintaining readability.\n662\t- **OpenClaude's guard hooks**: Shell-level security hooks (`guard.sh`, `guard-write.sh`) that enforce restrictions at the subprocess boundary, independent of the bot code.\n663\t- **terranc's numbered-option-to-inline-keyboard conversion**: When Claude presents numbered choices, they're automatically converted to tappable Telegram buttons.\n664\t- **Official plugin's pairing code flow**: A user-friendly alternative to requiring users to find their numeric Telegram ID.\n665\t- **heyagent's message batching**: Messages arriving during active execution are grouped and sent as one batch in the next run, reducing both API calls and context noise.\n666\t- **Claude-to-IM's no-bundled-persistence design**: Forces the host application to implement storage, ensuring data ownership is explicit rather than implicit.\n667\t\n668\t#### Common Trade-offs\n669\t\n670\t| Trade-off | Projects choosing A | Projects choosing B |\n671\t|-----------|-------------------|-------------------|\n672\t| **Polling vs webhooks** | Polling: terranc, ccbot, heyagent, Claude-to-IM (simpler, no HTTP exposure) | Webhooks: hanxiao, RichardAtCT/API (lower latency, requires public endpoint) |\n673\t| **SDK vs CLI** | SDK: most projects (richer integration, session management) | CLI: viniciustodesco, ccbot/hanxiao via tmux (simpler, fewer dependencies, preserves native auth) |\n674\t| **Single-user vs multi-user** | Single-user: andrueandersoncs, heyagent (simpler security model) | Multi-user: RichardAtCT, telclaude, n3d1117 (complex but shareable) |\n675\t| **Stateful vs stateless** | Stateful with SQLite/JSON: RichardAtCT, ccbot, andrueandersoncs | Stateless/in-memory: telegram-cli-bridge, n3d1117 (simpler but loses state on restart) |\n676\t| **MarkdownV2 vs HTML** | MarkdownV2: claudegram (richer formatting) | HTML: ccbot, Claude-to-IM (avoids escaping nightmares) |\n677\t\n678\t#### Common Pitfalls (from Issues and Documentation)\n679\t\n680\t- **Telegram MarkdownV2 escaping**: Multiple projects document this as a major pain point. Special characters must be escaped, and nested formatting (bold inside code blocks) is error-prone.\n681\t- **Telegram message edit rate limits**: Progressive streaming via message edits can hit Telegram's rate limits. Debouncing/throttling is essential.\n682\t- **No message history in Telegram Bot API**: Bots cannot retrieve past messages. Photos must be downloaded eagerly on arrival. The official plugin explicitly documents this limitation.\n683\t- **Claude Code session lifecycle**: Sessions can expire, hit token limits, or crash. Projects that don't handle context handoff gracefully lose conversation continuity.\n684\t- **Concurrent request ordering**: Without a queue, rapid messages can be processed out of order or create race conditions.\n685\t- **macOS sleep**: Several projects implement \"caffeinate\" or sleep prevention because the host machine going to sleep kills the bridge.\n686\t\n687\t---\n688\t\n689\t## Architecture Taxonomy\n690\t\n691\t```\n692\t                    +-----------------+\n693\t                    |    Telegram     |\n694\t                    |   Bot API       |\n695\t                    +--------+--------+\n696\t                             |\n697\t              +--------------+--------------+\n698\t              |              |              |\n699\t         Polling        Webhooks       MCP Channel\n700\t              |              |              |\n701\t    +---------+-----+   +---+---+   +------+------+\n702\t    | Bot Process    |   | HTTP  |   | Claude Code |\n703\t    | (Python/TS/JS) |   | Server|   | --channels  |\n704\t    +-------+--------+   +---+---+   +------+------+\n705\t            |                 |              |\n706\t    +-------+--------+-------+------+-------+\n707\t    |                |              |\n708\t    v                v              v\n709\t+--------+    +-----------+   +--------+\n710\t| Claude |    | Claude    |   | Claude |\n711\t| Code   |    | Code CLI  |   | Agent  |\n712\t| SDK    |    | subprocess|   | SDK    |\n713\t+--------+    +-----------+   | (MCP)  |\n714\t                              +--------+\n715\t    |                |\n716\t    v                v\n717\t+--------+    +-----------+\n718\t| Direct |    | tmux      |\n719\t| API    |    | send-keys |\n720\t+--------+    +-----------+\n721\t```\n722\t\n723\t### Integration Method Comparison\n724\t\n725\t| Method | Pros | Cons | Used by |\n726\t|--------|------|------|---------|\n727\t| **Claude Code SDK** | Rich session management, tool visibility, streaming events | Tight coupling to SDK version, Node.js dependency | terranc, RichardAtCT, telclaude, claudegram, OpenClaude |\n728\t| **CLI subprocess** | Simple, preserves native auth, language-agnostic | Parsing JSON output, limited session control | viniciustodesco |\n729\t| **tmux bridge** | Terminal stays source of truth, zero SDK dependency, desktop/mobile seamless | Fragile screen-scraping, limited structured data, 2s polling latency | ccbot, hanxiao |\n730\t| **MCP Channel** | Official Anthropic integration, native plugin system | Locked to Claude Code's plugin lifecycle, limited customization | Official plugin |\n731\t| **Task API** | Decoupled, supports multiple CLI tools | Extra infrastructure (task-api server), added latency | telegram-cli-bridge |\n732\t\n733\t---\n734\t\n735\t## Key Takeaways\n736\t\n737\t### What Almost Everyone Does\n738\t\n739\t1. **Telegram user ID allowlist** for authentication\n740\t2. **Environment variables** for configuration\n741\t3. **Polling** for Telegram updates (not webhooks)\n742\t4. **Progressive message editing** for streaming responses\n743\t5. **Voice transcription** via Whisper (OpenAI or Groq)\n744\t6. **Session persistence** of some form (SQLite, JSON, or Claude's `--resume`)\n745\t\n746\t### What Only the Best Do\n747\t\n748\t1. **Tiered permission systems** with per-tool granularity (telclaude)\n749\t2. **LLM-based semantic screening** of incoming messages (telclaude)\n750\t3. **Credential vault isolation** where agents never see raw API keys (telclaude)\n751\t4. **Code-block-aware message chunking** (ccbot, claudegram)\n752\t5. **Watchdog/supervisor processes** with circuit breakers (terranc, OpenClaude)\n753\t6. **Comprehensive test suites** (OpenClaude: 218 tests, RichardAtCT: 143+ tests)\n754\t7. **Audit logging** with token redaction (telclaude, Claude-to-IM)\n755\t8. **Guard hooks** enforcing security at the subprocess boundary (OpenClaude)\n756\t9. **Telegraph Instant View** for long-form content (claudegram)\n757\t10. **Fail-closed security defaults** (telclaude)\n758\t\n759\t### Gaps and Opportunities\n760\t\n761\t1. **Prompt injection defense is weak across the board.** Only telclaude implements LLM pre-screening. Most projects rely solely on user allowlists, which doesn't protect against a legitimate user's compromised device sending malicious input.\n762\t2. **No project implements E2E encryption** between Telegram and the bridge. Messages transit Telegram's servers in plaintext.\n763\t3. **Cost tracking is rare** in Claude-specific bridges. The ChatGPT bots (n3d1117, FlamingoFiesta) are more mature here.\n764\t4. **Multi-device session continuity** (e.g., starting a conversation on phone, continuing on desktop Telegram) is not explicitly handled by any project.\n765\t5. **Structured logging** (JSON-format logs suitable for log aggregation) is not implemented by any project.\n766\t6. **Health check endpoints** for monitoring infrastructure integration are absent from most projects.\n767\t7. **Graceful shutdown** (draining active requests before exiting) is rarely documented.\n768\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"call_a8f62a5469d24fbb8805b6ca","session_id":"3615c053-5998-4a9a-b6a0-573d164a84dd","uuid":"df04f10e-b26d-482f-8b65-8548140efd91","timestamp":"2026-05-04T04:07:45.189Z"}

Proxy-internal errors use:\n36\t\n37\t| Code | Meaning |\n38\t|---|---|\n39\t| 502 | Telegram API unreachable |\n40\t| 503 | Proxy not connected to Telegram (polling not started) |\n41\t| 504 | Telegram API timeout |\n42\t\n43\t---\n44\t\n45\t## Endpoints\n46\t\n47\t### GET /health\n48\t\n49\tHealth check. No parameters.\n50\t\n51\t**Response 200:**\n52\t```json\n53\t{\n54\t  \"ok\": true,\n55\t  \"polling\": true,\n56\t  \"last_update_id\": 123456789,\n57\t  \"uptime_seconds\": 3600\n58\t}\n59\t```\n60\t\n61\t| Field | Type | Description |\n62\t|---|---|---|\n63\t| `ok` | boolean | Proxy is operational |\n64\t| `polling` | boolean | Actively polling Telegram |\n65\t| `last_update_id` | integer \\| null | Most recent update_id received |\n66\t| `uptime_seconds` | integer | Seconds since proxy started |\n67\t\n68\t---\n69\t\n70\t### GET /updates\n71\t\n72\tLong-polls Telegram and returns pending updates as normalized envelopes.\n73\t\n74\t**Query parameters:**\n75\t\n76\t| Param | Type | Default | Description |\n77\t|---|---|---|---|\n78\t| `timeout` | integer | 30 | Long-poll timeout in seconds. Proxy adds 5s to its Telegram poll to ensure the Telegram response arrives first. |\n79\t\n80\t**Response 200:**\n81\t```json\n82\t{\n83\t  \"ok\": true,\n84\t  \"updates\": [ <Update> ... ]\n85\t}\n86\t```\n87\t\n88\tThe bridge should call this in a loop. The proxy tracks `offset` internally — each call returns only new updates since the last acknowledged batch. Updates are acknowledged implicitly when the bridge fetches the next batch.\n89\t\n90\t#### Update envelope\n91\t\n92\tEvery Telegram update is normalized into this envelope. The proxy strips all auth context and resolves file references.\n93\t\n94\t```json\n95\t{\n96\t  \"update_id\": 123456789,\n97\t  \"type\": \"message | edited_message | callback_query | service\",\n98\t  \"chat_id\": -1001234567890,\n99\t  \"thread_id\": 42,\n100\t  \"from_user\": {\n101\t    \"id\": 789,\n102\t    \"first_name\": \"Jed\",\n103\t    \"username\": \"jedarden\"\n104\t  },\n105\t  \"message_id\": 1001,\n106\t  \"timestamp\": 1712169600,\n107\t  \"content\": { <ContentObject> },\n108\t  \"reply_to_message_id\": 999,\n109\t  \"service\": null\n110\t}\n111\t```\n112\t\n113\t| Field | Type | Required | Description |\n114\t|---|---|---|---|\n115\t| `update_id` | integer | yes | Telegram update ID |\n116\t| `type` | string | yes | One of: `message`, `edited_message`, `callback_query`, `service` |\n117\t| `chat_id` | integer | yes | Telegram chat/group ID |\n118\t| `thread_id` | integer \\| null | no | Forum topic ID. `null` = General topic |\n119\t| `from_user` | object | yes | Sender info |\n120\t| `from_user.id` | integer | yes | Telegram user ID |\n121\t| `from_user.first_name` | string | yes | User's first name |\n122\t| `from_user.username` | string \\| null | no | User's @username |\n123\t| `message_id` | integer | yes | Telegram message ID (for replies/edits) |\n124\t| `timestamp` | integer | yes | Unix epoch of the message |\n125\t| `content` | object | yes | Message content (see Content types below) |\n126\t| `reply_to_message_id` | integer \\| null | no | Message ID this is replying to |\n127\t| `service` | object \\| null | no | Service message data (see Service types below) |\n128\t\n129\t#### Content types\n130\t\n131\tThe `content` object has a `type` discriminator field.\n132\t\n133\t**Text:**\n134\t```json\n135\t{\n136\t  \"type\": \"text\",\n137\t  \"text\": \"refactor the error handling in main.py\",\n138\t  \"entities\": [\n139\t    {\n140\t      \"type\": \"bot_command\",\n141\t      \"offset\": 0,\n142\t      \"length\": 7,\n143\t      \"extra\": null\n144\t    }\n145\t  ]\n146\t}\n147\t```\n148\t\n149\t| Field | Type | Description |\n150\t|---|---|---|\n151\t| `type` | `\"text\"` | Discriminator |\n152\t| `text` | string | Message text (1–4096 chars) |\n153\t| `entities` | array | Telegram MessageEntity objects, preserved as-is |\n154\t\n155\t**Photo:**\n156\t```json\n157\t{\n158\t  \"type\": \"photo\",\n159\t  \"file_id\": \"AgACAgIAAxk...\",\n160\t  \"width\": 800,\n161\t  \"height\": 600,\n162\t  \"file_size\": 52400,\n163\t  \"caption\": \"screenshot of the error\",\n164\t  \"caption_entities\": []\n165\t}\n166\t```\n167\t\n168\t| Field | Type | Description |\n169\t|---|---|---|\n170\t| `type` | `\"photo\"` | Discriminator |\n171\t| `file_id` | string | Proxy-scoped file reference (use with `GET /file`) |\n172\t| `width` | integer | Pixel width of selected resolution |\n173\t| `height` | integer | Pixel height of selected resolution |\n174\t| `file_size` | integer \\| null | Size in bytes |\n175\t| `caption` | string \\| null | Photo caption |\n176\t| `caption_entities` | array | Entities in caption |\n177\t\n178\tThe proxy selects the best resolution that does not exceed 1280px on the long edge. If only smaller sizes exist, the largest is used.\n179\t\n180\t**Voice:**\n181\t```json\n182\t{\n183\t  \"type\": \"voice\",\n184\t  \"file_id\": \"AwACAgIAAxk...\",\n185\t  \"duration\": 12,\n186\t  \"file_size\": 19200,\n187\t  \"mime_type\": \"audio/ogg\"\n188\t}\n189\t```\n190\t\n191\t| Field | Type | Description |\n192\t|---|---|---|\n193\t| `type` | `\"voice\"` | Discriminator |\n194\t| envelope differs slightly:\n294\t\n295\t```json\n296\t{\n297\t  \"update_id\": 123456790,\n298\t  \"type\": \"callback_query\",\n299\t  \"chat_id\": -1001234567890,\n300\t  \"thread_id\": 42,\n301\t passed to `POST /answer_callback` |\n318\t| `content.data` | string | The `callback_data` from the inline button (1–64 bytes) |\n319\t\n320\t`message_id` refers to the message containing the inline keyboard that was pressed.\n321\t\n322\t#### Service types\n323\t\n324\tWhen `type` is `service`, the `content` is `null` and the `service` field is populated:\n325\t\n326\t```json\n327\t{\n328\t  \"type\": \"service\",\n329\t  \"chat_id\": -1001234567890,\n330\t  \"thread_id\": 42,\n331\t  \"from_user\": { \"id\": 789, \"first_name\": \"Jed\", \"username\": \"jedarden\" },\n332\t middleware\",\n338\t    \"icon_color\": 7322096\n339\t  }\n340\t}\n341\t```\n342\t\n343\t| Service type | Fields | Description |\n344\t|---|---|---|\n345\t| `forum_topic_created` | `name`, `icon_color`, `icon_custom_emoji_id` | New topic created |\n346\t| `forum_topic_edited` | `name`, `icon_color`, `icon_custom_emoji_id` | Topic name/icon changed |\n347\t| `forum_topic_closed` | (none) `member: {id, first_name, username}` | User left |\n351\t\n352\t---\n353\t\n354\t### GET /file/{file_id}\n355\t\n356\tDownload a file that was referenced in an update's `file_id` field.\n357\t\n358\t**Path parameters:**\n359\t\n360\t| Param | Type | Description |\n361\t|---|---|---|\n362\t| `file_id` | string | The `file_id` from a content object |\n363\t\n364\t**Response 200:**\n365\t- `Content-Type`: the file's MIME type (e.g., `image/jpeg`, `audio/ogg`, `application/pdf`)\n366\t- `Content-Length`: file size in bytes\n367\t- `Content-Disposition`: `attachment; filename=\"<original_filename>\"` (if available)\n368\t- Body: raw file bytes\n369\t\n370\t**Response 404:**\n371\t```json\n372\t{\n373\t  \"ok\": false,\n374\t  \"error_code\": 404,\n375\t  \"description\": \"File not found or expired\"\n376\t}\n377\t```\n378\t\n379\t**Response 413:**\n380\t```json\n381\t{\n382\t  \"ok\": false,\n383\t  \"error_code\": 413,\n384\t  \"description\": \"File exceeds 20MB download limit\"\n385\t}\n386\t```\n387\t\n388\tNote: Telegram file links expire after ~1 hour. The proxy fetches from Telegram on each request — it does not cache files. The bridge should download files promptly after receiving the update.\n389\t\n390\t---\n391\t\n392\t### POST /send\n393\t\n394\tSend a text message.\n395\t\n396\t**Request body:**\n397\t```json\n398\t{\n399\t  \"chat_id\": -1001234567890,\n400\t  \"thread_id\": 42,\n401\t  \"text\": \"Here is the refactored code...\",\n402\t  \"parse_mode\": \"HTML\",\n403\t  \"reply_to_message_id\": 1001,\n404\t  \"reply_markup\": null\n405\t}\n406\t```\n407\t\n408\t| Field | Type | Required | Description |\n409\t|---|---|---|---|\n410\t| `chat_id` | integer | yes | Target chat |\n411\t| `thread_id` | integer | no | Target forum topic. Omit for plain text |\n414\t| `reply_to_message_id` | integer | no | Message to reply to |\n415\t| `reply_markup` | object | no | Inline keyboard (see Inline Keyboard below) |\n416\t\n417\t**Response 200:**\n418\t```json\n419\t{\n420\t  \"ok\": true,\n421\t  \"message_id\": 2001\n422\t}\n423\t```\n424\t\n425\tThe proxy returns the `message_id` of the sent message. The bridge stores this for subsequent edits.\n426\t\n427\t---\n428\t\n429\t### POST /edit\n430\t\n431\tEdit an existing text message. press. Must be called within 10 seconds of receiving the callback query.\n748\t\n749\t**Request body:**\n750\t```json\n751\t{\n752\t  \"callback_query_id\": \"abc123def456\",\n753\t  \"text\": \"Tool approved\",\n754\t  \"show_alert\": false\n755\t}\n756\t```\n757\t\n758\t| Field | Type | Required | Description |\n759\t|---|---|---|---|\n760\t| `callback_query_id` | string | yes | From the callback content in the update |\n761\t| `text` | string | no | Notification text shown to user (0–200 chars) |\n762\t| `show_alert` | boolean | no | Show as alert dialog vs. top notification |\n763\t\n764\t**Response 200:**\n765\t```json\n766\t{\n767\t  \"ok\": true\n768\t}\n769\t```\n770\t\n771\t---\n772\t\n773\t## Inline Keyboard Schema\n774\t\n775\tUsed in `reply_markup` field of `/send` and `/edit`.\n776\t\n777\t```json\n778\t{\n779\t  \"inline_keyboard\": [\n780\t    [\n781\t      { \"text\": \"Approve\", \"callback_data\": \"approve_tool_abc123\" },\n782\t      { \"text\": \"Deny\", \"callback_data\": \"deny_tool_abc123\" }\n783\t    ],\n784\t    [\n785\t      { \"text\": \"View Details\", \"callback_data\": \"details_tool_abc123\" }\n786\t    ]\n787\t  ]\n788\t}\n789\t```\n790\t\n791\t- Outer array = rows, inner arrays = buttons per row\n792\t- `callback_data` is 1–64 bytes, returned in the callback query update\n793\t- The bridge encodes session and action context into `callback_data`\n794\t\n795\t---\n796\t\n797\t## Rate Limiting\n798\t\n799\tThe proxy enforces Telegram's rate limits and returns 429 with retry information:\n800\t\n801\t```json\n802\t{\n803\t  \"ok\": false,\n804\t  \"error_code\": 429,\n805\t  \"description\": \"Too Many Requests: retry after 3\",\n806\t  \"retry_after\": 3\n807\t}\n808\t```\n809\t\n810\tThe bridge must respect `retry_after` and not retry before that interval.\n811\t\n812\tTelegram rate limits:\n813\t- 1 message/second per chat (private)\n814\t- 20 messages/minute per group\n815\t- ~30 requests/second global across all chats\n816\t\n817\tThe bridge is responsible for debouncing edit calls (max 1/second) and queuing sends per chat.\n818\t\n819\t---\n820\t\n821\t## Versioning\n822\t\n823\tThe contract version is returned in the `/health` response and can be checked on startup:\n824\t\n825\t```json\n826\t{\n827\t  \"ok\": true,\n828\t  \"polling\": true,\n829\t  \"last_update_id\": 123456789,\n830\t  \"uptime_seconds\": 3600,\n831\t  \"contract_version\": \"1.0\"\n832\t}\n833\t```\n834\t\n835\tBreaking changes increment the major version. The bridge should check `contract_version` on startup and warn if mismatched.\n836\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"call_a8f62a5469d24fbb8805b6ca","session_id":"3615c053-5998-4a9a-b6a0-573d164a84dd","uuid":"9d21d439-a3e8-4a71-8402-1d6df2ca7ef9","timestamp":"2026-05-04T04:07:50.020Z"}
